accounting: what stands behind it:
accounting:   of the rounds with a closeness term 0 rest on a lemma of the paper that this protocol builds on (see THIRD_PARTY.md), proved there (within a third of a code's distance; the same bound is also in other papers: blocks/numbers/codes.h names them)
accounting:   127 of them rest on the theorem on codes, Theorem 4.6 of ePrint 2025/2055, which we take as given and of which we have verified no proof ourselves (blocks/numbers/codes.h says what the posting of November 6, 2025 prints behind it): TAKEN AS GIVEN
accounting:   beyond a third a fold's round has no factor for the number of interleaved rows (a bound argued on paper for this protocol, for linear codes over E and two tables combined by one challenge; see docs/limits.md)

accounting:   the bounds for this protocol's own steps are argued on paper, in a technical report that is not public and not refereed: see docs/limits.md#what-the-theorem-assumes
accounting:   the smallness test's round counts one column: the first that no small integer column explains, which is fixed before the draw (argued on paper for this protocol; see docs/limits.md); a union bound over all columns stands among the stricter readings
accounting:   the blocks' round errors and the compiled bound are cited from published work, some of it not peer-reviewed, or argued on paper in a technical report that is not public and not refereed; they are not proved for these programs (the Lean library proves its own verifier's bound, not this one)
accounting:   111 round(s) have an error that is computed from a number which the parameter file or this program's own code supplies and which no check ties to the run at that round (blocks/numbers/plan.h, ON_THE_CALLERS_WORD, says which numbers; some are held at a later place; see docs/limits.md#how-the-programs-count). Not counted: the rule and the integer m under which a group's distance stands: they are the analysis's choice and no number of a run; that all rounds of one chain on the same two codes were priced under one choice is not asked by this account: the committer, the prover and the verifier ask it of each chain when they read the public files
accounting:   that g bits of work before a message multiply its round's error by 2^-g is assumed for this program's hash chains; it is proved for another setting in ePrint 2021/582, Theorem 6
accounting:   that the programs compute the protocol is not proved; no figure here is a security level
accounting: hiding is a separate bound, in the numbers of queries and of proofs on one commitment, and is no part of these figures
accounting: FOR INFORMATION, stricter readings of the same rounds, which decide nothing. Each starts from the rounds' errors as they are charged above; in some rounds the charge is the largest of several events' chances and not their sum: so none is a union bound over all events
